## Read or resolved here, and removed

- **826 — British agent at Port Sainte-Marie → the Duke of Newcastle, c. July 1729 (Huntington mssBLA 191 p. 5)**: key recovered from adjacent plaintext, 28 Oct 2026. Code table rebuilt from the interlinear decipherments on the sibling letters mssBLA 187/188 and the 14 July, 29 June and later letters; 134 of 141 groups read. See targets/portstemarie1729/.
- **651 — Joel R. Poinsett → James Monroe, 14 June 1814 (NARA M37 roll 2 frames 0097-0100)**: read from existing decipherment, 28 Oct 2026. The clerk's interlinear decipherment is on the original (frame 0098) and Manning printed it in 1925 (vol. 1 doc. 201); both copies transcribed, 122 of 123 groups. Its code is the key of the 1813 Santiago letter (no. 639, still open); write-up poinsett1813.
- **653 — Powhatan Ellis → John Forsyth, Mexico City despatches of 1836 and No. 9 of 16 Nov 1839 (NARA M97 rolls 8–10)**: read from existing decipherment, 27 Oct 2026. Michael Brackx (issue #120) found the Department's decipherments filmed with every coded despatch but 26 Oct 1836, which Bosch García (1984) prints; WAINUT (PR #107) transcribed No. 9 and aligned it with its clear copy. The groups decode with the JQA table (`targets/jqa/code_table.tsv`); 14 disputed 1839 groups settled on the frames, and 26 Oct 1836 continues onto frame 0324 (three groups). Write-up `ellis1839`.
- **409 — Venceslau de Lima → King Manuel II, code telegrams of Nov-Dec 1909 (ANTT PT/TT/CAM/M09)**: read from existing decipherment, 27 Oct 2026 (WAINUT, PR #105). The receiver pencilled the decode above the groups; eight coded telegrams on the King's state visit to Spain, France and England and his return journey, 400 of 409 code items read; 261 values of a two-part code paired. See targets/lima1909/.
- **629 — Gen. Joseph E. Johnston → Secretary of War Seddon, Jackson, 16 Jun 1863 (NARA RG 109 naId 3854726)**: existing decipherment found, 27 Oct 2026. The War Department's "Translation of Cypher" is image 3 of the item; the text is printed in 1864 and in OR I 24 pt. 1 p. 227. The key MANCHESTERBLUFF fits when it advances by plaintext length (26/33); ISDX is ISLX. Contributed by WAINUT. See [`targets/johnston1863/`](targets/johnston1863/).
- **644 — Rose O'Neal Greenhow, letter in cipher on mourning paper, 1861 (NARA RG 59 naId 1634036)**: deciphered in part on 27 Oct 2026 with the key found on her body in 1864 (NARA M1091 naId 62017285 image 0484, not 0483). Side 1 about 60% of words as sense; side 2 and the figure scrap open. Contributed by WAINUT. See [`targets/greenhow1861/`](targets/greenhow1861/).
- **645 and 646 — Thomas Jordan → "My Dearest Rachel" (Greenhow), 1861 (NARA naId 1634051 images 1-2 and naId 6925618)**: one letter, resolved 27 Oct 2026. 646 is the cipher original (Jordan → Greenhow, not Greenhow → unknown); 645 images 1-2 are its clear copy; the 1864 Greenhow key fits the cipher unchanged. Contributed by WAINUT. See [`targets/jordan1861/`](targets/jordan1861/).
- **617 — De Puebla → Miguel Pérez de Almazán, London, 21 Apr 1506, AGS Patronato Real leg. 54 doc. 78 (PARES 2208120)**: read in part on 27 Oct 2026 with Tomokiyo's reconstruction of Puebla's First Cipher (contributed by WAINUT, PR #101); the cipher passages of ll. 18–29 read as sentences, ll. 11–17 and the clear text open. Shelfmark corrected: leg. 54 doc. 78, not "Patronato Real (not yet opened)". [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/puebla1506.html)
- **616 — Ferdinand and Isabella → De Puebla, Almazán, 21 Jun 1496, ciphered article, AGS Patronato Real leg. 52 doc. 47 f. 396**: resolved 27 Oct 2026 (WAINUT, PR #101). Deciphered at the time (the minuta carries the article in clear, note on f. 397v) and calendared by Bergenroth, CSP Spain I no. 138; the unit also holds the clear drafts nos. 137 and 141. A three-line sample decoded with the extant key PTR leg. 52 doc. 33; no page (targets/puebla1496/).
- **636 — Robert R. Livingston → John Jay, Philadelphia, 28 Nov 1781, "Make this addition to your cypher" (ALS NHi Jay Papers EJ 598)**: existing decipherment found, 27 Oct 2026. The line the Selected Papers of John Jay call undecipherable is glossed in Livingston's draft and entered on Jay's copy of the Thomson code: be, ce, de, fe, ge, he = 600-605 (226 = e, not r). See [`targets/livingston1781/`](targets/livingston1781/).
- **380 — João IV → the marquês de Nisa, ambassador in Paris, ANTT Manuscritos da Livraria 1017, letters 9, 13, 44, 49, 56, 153, 1642–44**: read in part on 27 Oct 2026, key recovered from adjacent plaintext. Alphabet A rebuilt from the letter-book's own clear copies (letters 8, 9, 13); alphabet B (Greek-shaped signs, 1644-45) from the volume's list of deciphered passages (m0276) against two 1645 siblings. 398 of 438 cipher words read; the 1643 runs illegible at 1060 px; five words of letter 153 open. Write-up: [joao1642](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/joao1642.html).
- **542 — Le Mercier de La Rivière and La Touche (Martinique) → the Navy minister (Berryer), ANOM COL C8 A 64 f° 1, 12 Jun 1761**: read in part on 27 Oct 2026, key recovered from adjacent plaintext. The two-part code was rebuilt from the interlinear decipherment of the sibling letter C8 A 63 f° 208 (21 Aug 1761) and extended from context; 754 of 818 groups read as sense (92.2%), about 40 single-use code words open. Write-up mercier1761.
- **484 — Jefferson-era legation codes Nos. 10, 13 and 16, residual groups (Short 26 Jul 1792, Carmichael 14 Apr 1788, Thomas Pinckney 28 Aug 1793), LoC Jefferson Papers ser. 1 and NARA M30 roll 2**: existing decipherment checked, 27 Oct 2026. 737 code groups transcribed, 720 with a reading (97.7%); the editors' readings confirmed from contemporary decodings in sibling letters; Pinckney's 'undeciphered 497' is 447; open: Short 401 ×2, 1083, 359 199 34 724, Pinckney 447, Carmichael 597, each a single occurrence. See [targets/legation1788/](targets/legation1788/). [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/legation1788.html)
- **630 — Cornwallis ↔ Nisbet Balfour intercepted cipher letters, NARA RG 360, Papers of the Continental Congress item 51 vol. 1, pp. 717–736**: read from the existing decipherment on 27 Oct 2026. Balfour → Cornwallis, Nos. 1–3 and a second letter (March 1781), and Balfour → Stewart (12 Aug 1781), each with a 1781 interlinear decipherment; the Common cipher's ring and setting rule and the Stewart table rebuilt from it, all numbers deciphered again (99.8%). The Cornwallis → Wemyss/Balfour and Craig → Cornwallis passages have translations on pp. 759–761, not transcribed. [`targets/balfour1781/`](targets/balfour1781/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/balfour1781.html)
- **631 — Papers of the Continental Congress, Miscellaneous Papers (PCC item 59 vol. 1), 'communications in cipher' filed with the keys of Franklin, Jay and Dumas**: closed, already deciphered, 27 Oct 2026. The seven cipher pieces are the office's own encipherment worksheets and attested copies with the English on the page, or are printed: Livingston → Franklin 26 Nov 1781 (worksheet pp. 325-328 + 169-172; Papers of Benjamin Franklin 36), Committee for Foreign Affairs → Jay 16 June 1780 (Jay Papers 2), Thomson's dictionary-code note of 19 May 1780 (decoded in Jay's hand, Columbia), and resolves of 10 Aug 1781, 22 Jan, 8 Feb and 7 Aug 1782 (Journals). One variant against the Franklin edition: the worksheet has "one Month's continuance" where the edition prints "one years". Notes only (targets/pcc1780/NOTES.md), no write-up.
- **618 — Rodrigo González de Puebla (London) → King Ferdinand, 15 Apr 1507, AGS Patronato Real, Tratados con Inglaterra leg. 5 ff. 31–32 (PARES 2208061, probably f. 31)**: already in print, 27 Oct 2026. Puebla wrote two letters that day: f. 31, wholly in cipher, was deciphered at the time by Almazán; f. 32 is a short clear duplicate with a few cipher words. Both are calendared in Bergenroth, CSP Spain I (1862) nos. 511–512, pp. 408–410 (the sweep's "no Puebla letter in April 1507" was wrong), and printed in full Spanish with the remaining cipher words deciphered in the 1868 Supplement, nos. 14–15, pp. 90–99. Images not seen (PARES 429). Entry 617's PARES id corrected to 2208120. Notes only (targets/puebla1507/NOTES.md), no write-up.
- **569 — Count Georg Ernst zu Erbach → Landgrave Ludwig VI of Hesse-Darmstadt, HStAD D 4 Nr. 248/3 (39 letters, 1654–1670)**: key recovered from adjacent plaintext on 27 Oct 2026. Only three 1666 letters carry cipher (images 003, 004, 006): number pairs giving line and letter of a rhymed German Lord's Prayer, rebuilt from a contemporary Latin outline filmed beside the long letter; all three deciphered (591 of 610 pairs as the key gives them). Not the Auflösungen in D 4 Nr. 239/1, which hold a 1640s Georg II table. [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/erbach1653.html)
- **418 — Antoni Augustyn Deboli → King Stanisław August, cipher despatches from St Petersburg, 1789, AGAD Zbiór Popielów 17**: deciphered at the time, 27 Oct 2026. The cabinet's Polish decipherments are the same-date texts in ZbPop 419 (103 of 106; 3 in ZbPop 417). The 6 Jan pair was aligned in the 1790 code (1559 = w, 951 = od, repeated 355 and 1567 at Hiszpańskiego/Hiszpanij and wyiednała/wypuszczenie). Then (27–28 Oct 2026, targets/deboli1789) eleven pairs aligned, the code rebuilt (630 groups) and the three despatches without a clear text read in part: 5,094 of 5,903 groups. Write-up deboli1789. [`targets/deboli1789/`](targets/deboli1789/) · [`targets/deboli1790/`](targets/deboli1790/)
- **420 — Antoni Augustyn Deboli → King Stanisław August, cipher despatches from St Petersburg, 1791, AGAD Zbiór Popielów 5**: deciphered at the time, 27 Oct 2026. The cabinet's Polish decipherments are the same-date texts in ZbPop 421 (98 of 105). The 7 Jan pair was aligned: "ani od WKMci, ani od" = 1944 951 1570 1944 951, as in 1790. Notes only. [`targets/deboli1790/`](targets/deboli1790/) Five despatches transcribed and aligned group by group with their decipherments (98.9% of 3,082 groups; two codes): [`targets/deboli1791/`](targets/deboli1791/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/deboli1791.html)
- **264 (R1871 part) — Sebastiano Foscarini (Paris) to the Doge, despatch no. 117, 11 Sept 1680, ASVe Busta 30 f. 4 (DECODE R1871)**: read from existing decipherment, 27 Oct 2026; removed. Paolo Bonavoglia's decipherment (crittologia.eu/storia/SebFoscarini_Parigi16800911.html; original filza 171A, chancery decipherment filza 166) was offline during the attempt here (22 Sept 2026, ciphertext-only annealing failed) and is back online; Brice Dudout reported it and rebuilt the table (five-code syllabary blocks, out of order). [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r1871.html), [targets/r1871/](targets/r1871/NOTES.md).
- **391 — Afonso VI (countersigned Castelo Melhor) to the conde de Vila Flor, ANTT Luís de Miranda Pereira liv. 2 (PT/TT/LMP/0002)**: read by Brice Dudout, 27 Oct 2026, not here; removed. The cipher letters are of May 1663, not 1660-61: four letters read at 97-99% (13, 15 and 26 May 1663 and a note of 15 May), key checked on a held-out letter, on duplicate copies and on a sentence clear in one copy and enciphered in the other; leaves m0057 and m0059 are swapped in the volume. Not in Ericeira. Europe chiffrée, documents.html#vilaflor-1663.
- **498 — Ragusa intelligence to Ferdinand I (Caraffa, Ferducci), HHStA Türkei I 1-5-12 to 2-2-24, 1530-32**: resolved, 27 Oct 2026 (Brice Dudout), not here; removed. Every piece but 2-2-19 has a contemporary gloss; Dudout read 2-2-19 (Ferducci, Fiume, 19 July 1532) with codes rebuilt from the glosses of 2-2-9 and 2-2-24, agreeing 6/6 with an independent 1542 table (catalogue 499). Ferducci offers to seize the French ambassador at sea, probably Antonio Rincon. Europe chiffrée, documents.html#ferducci-1532.
- **439 — Ludwik Wąsowicz → Aleksander Michał Lubomirski, ANK AKPot 3227 (DECODE R7501–R7511), 1662–63**: read by Brice Dudout, 27 Oct 2026, not here; removed. A simple substitution, one two-digit number per letter, read with the nineteenth-century table R7501 and checked blind on the letter of 5 May 1663 (R7508); seven letters of 1662–63, not 1662–69. R7510 is the end of the Jarosław letter of 9 Dec 1663 (not 1665) that begins in R7509; R7502 is already deciphered between the lines and on the sheet R7511. Read from DECODE images shared for study; none reproduced. Europe chiffrée, documents.html#krakow-1663.
- **626 — Lope Hurtado de Mendoza → Charles V, Tordesillas, 10 Dec 1520, AGS PTR leg. 2 doc. 36,8 (PARES 12988891)**: read by Brice Dudout, 27 Oct 2026, not here; removed. Wholly in cipher, sent the same day as the clear letter PTR leg. 2 doc. 36,6 that Danvila printed (MHE XXXVI pp. 688–690). Paz's slip points to a key in the letter of 23 Sept 1520 (36,2), which does not fit; Dudout rebuilt a homophonic letter substitution (about 35 signs, dotted sigla for persons) from the letter itself, checked it on held-out lines and read about 97.7% of some 920 cipher words. The sigla other than ·fre· (probably the Admiral) are unidentified. Europe chiffrée, documents.html#lope-hurtado-1520.
- **485 — Francis Dana → Robert R. Livingston, St Petersburg, 6/17 Apr 1783, PCC item 89 vol. II p. 730**: read independently by WAINUT and by Brice Dudout, 27 Oct 2026; removed. WAINUT recovered the three shifted alphabets from the ciphertext alone and read the eight lines in part, four code groups open ([`targets/dana1783/`](targets/dana1783/), in progress). Dudout broke it the same way, then found Dana's own table of October 1782 in the same volume (p. 656: alphabets from n, w and i, a nomenclator numbered 30–305), which reads all eight lines. Europe chiffrée, documents.html#dana-1783.
- **638 — Alexander H. Everett → Henry Clay, Madrid, 25 Sept 1825, "Notes of a conversation with Mr Zea", NARA M31 reel 27**: read independently by WAINUT and by Brice Dudout, 27 Oct 2026, both with the code table of targets/jqa; removed. WAINUT read the six lines Manning left in figures (pp. 14–15) and the passages of pp. 17–18, 347 of 355 groups ([`targets/everett1825/`](targets/everett1825/), in progress). Dudout read all of the cipher, pp. 1–6 included (74 + 1,127 groups, 0.986 of the second part), after checking the table on the despatch of 8 Sept 1825 against the Department's decoding (Manning doc. 1133): 288 of 304 groups agree. Europe chiffrée, documents.html#everett-1825.
- **652 — William H. Crawford → James Monroe, Paris, 15 Aug 1813 (not 3 Sept), NARA M34 roll 17 frames 0027–0029**: read independently by WAINUT and by Brice Dudout, 27 Oct 2026, both with the code table of targets/jqa; removed. The letter is item 1 of the legation's Despatch No. 3 (register, frame 0005: "Letter from Minister to Secret.y of State Aug. 15th 1813 in cypher", sent 19 Aug), and frame 0027 is headed "No (3)". WAINUT read 1,313 of 1,368 groups but dated the letter 3 Sept from the heading group 954 read as "3rd" ([`targets/crawford1813/`](targets/crawford1813/), in progress); Dudout read 1,330 of 1,371 groups and the heading 932 939 932 954 as the digits 1813. Despatch No. 4, Paris 3 Sept 1813 (frames 0031–0032), carries an interlinear decipherment, as do most of Crawford's other coded despatches in the volume. Europe chiffrée, documents.html#crawford-1813.
- **751 — Marmont → Jourdan, 1 June 1812, AHN Diversos-Colecciones 134 N.73**: read in part by Brice Dudout, 27 Oct 2026 (pull request #162, `targets/marmont1812/`); removed. The 1812 Grand Chiffre deciphering table (SHD "B n° 30", J.-F. Bouchaudy's photographs) applied unchanged; about 26 numbers unresolved (copying errors). With it, Jourdan → Soult, 28 May 1812 (N.34) read. [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/marmont1812.html)
- **724 — Colonel Jardet → Marshal Marmont, Paris, 6 March 1812, AHN Diversos-Colecciones 133 N.29**: resolved, 27 Oct 2026 (Brice Dudout, `targets/marmont1812/`); removed. The AHN piece is itself a decipherment of the time ("copia descifrada conforme"), with the Spanish reconstruction of the key attached: a homophonic cipher of 48 numbers on a line of Voltaire's Tancrède, not the Grand Chiffre of the other pieces.
- **419 — Antoni Augustyn Deboli → King Stanisław August, cipher despatches from St Petersburg, 1790, AGAD Zbiór Popielów 4**: deciphered at the time, 27 Oct 2026. All 109 despatches have the cabinet's Polish decipherment, filed apart: 2 Jan–2 Feb 1790 in ZbPop 417 k. 353–386, 5 Feb–31 Dec in ZbPop 420 (the "open despatches of the same day" of the entry). Paired by date and heading; code groups aligned on 2 Jan and 9 Feb (1559 = w, 1092 = po-, 606 = z). A Polish syllable-and-word code, not rebuilt. Notes only, no write-up. [`targets/deboli1790/`](targets/deboli1790/)
- **466 — Duke Frederik of Schleswig-Holstein ↔ Lübeck, 1523, Rigsarkivet Sønderjyske Fyrstearkiver, Hertug Frederik (1.), Sager vedrørende udlandet item 8 (Arkivalieronline epid 14908271, images 94–129)**: already in print, 26 Oct 2026. The "chifferskrift" is a name code: single signs for persons, places, days and troops in clear Low German. The key strip (Gemeinsch. Arch. cap. XIII, 38, endorsed by Wolf von Utenhof) is reproduced on the plate facing p. 312 of Schäfer and Techen, *Hanserecesse* III/8 (1910). That volume prints the coded letters (nn. 279–354) with every sign resolved; image 94 is its n. 292, witness K1 (XIII 47c). Key table in `targets/frederik1523/key.tsv`; notes only, no write-up.
- **483 — Jefferson's Code No. 11 (Barclay–Lamb code), Barclay, Carmichael, Short and Jefferson, 1786–1791, LoC Jefferson Papers ser. 1 and NARA M34 roll 2**: key recovered from adjacent plaintext on 27 Oct 2026, partial. 348 values rebuilt from Jefferson's interlinear decodings and clear-text sheets; 1,868 of 1,917 groups read; the editors' readings checked group by group ([sea] at 495 contradicted; Barclay's footnote = The Pro-cur-e-r [General?]). Open: the Adams P.S. (press copy illegible) and single-occurrence groups. [`targets/jefferson1787/`](targets/jefferson1787/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/jefferson1787.html)
- **481 — William Vans Murray → James Madison, Paris (not The Hague), 26 June 1801, NARA RG 59 Despatches Netherlands vol. 4 (M42 reel 5, frames 0397–0398)**: key recovered from adjacent plaintext on 26 Oct 2026. The coded P.S. is six groups (536. 406. 1004. 264. 1136. 1175); every value is decoded between the lines in Murray's 1797–98 despatches in the same code (M42 reel 4) and Wagner's 18 May 1801 decode: "As to the armed neutrality not a Word has yet been said". [`targets/murray1801/`](targets/murray1801/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/murray1801.html)
- **486 — William Smallwood → George Washington, Chester County, 19 Sept 1777 (ALS privately owned; Sprague transcript, LoC GW Papers)**: not a cipher, 26 Oct 2026. Five words erased on the original and replaced by *, §, §§, †, §; § stands for a number and for a route, so the marks are blanks, not a code, and the erased words are lost. `targets/smallwood1777/`. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/smallwood1777.html)
- **487 — Rufus King (London) → James Madison, 25 Apr and 8 June 1801, NARA RG 59 Despatches Great Britain vol. 9 (M30)**: already in print, 26 Oct 2026. Both despatches (nos. 14 and 21; the catalogue's "no. 15" was wrong) are printed in full in C. R. King, *Life and Correspondence of Rufus King* 3 (1896) 436–38 and 472–73, with the coded words in italics ("Italics in cipher"): 96 of 124 words of the 25 Apr final paragraph (George III's relapse) and "Lord Hawkesbury and Mr. Addington" on 8 June. The coded duplicates (M30 roll 9) are not online and were not seen; the code is not identified. Notes only (targets/king1801/NOTES.md), removed 26 Oct 2026.
- **480 — James C. Mountflorence → Madison (addressed to Marshall), Paris, 18 March 1801, NARA RG 59 Consular Despatches Paris (T1 roll 1, frame 0097)**: key recovered from the ciphertext alone on 26 Oct 2026, a one-part alphabetical code broken from four spelled names; all 39 groups decoded (the Louisiana retrocession for Tuscany); one Founders figure corrected (251, not 250). `targets/mountflorence1801/`.
- **225 (part) — F.G. van Dedem (Constantinople) → Van de Spiegel, R2122 (15 Jan 1789) and R2131 (9 Feb 1793)**: read on 23 Oct 2026 with the legation codebook filed under Kroll (Nationaal Archief 1.02.20 inv. 785), after the reading audit had left them decoded, not read; R2120 was read at the time (R2121). The entry is narrowed to R1895 (1799, another code). [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/dedem1788.html).
- **279 (part) — Keresdi Bethlen cipher letters, R4937, R4939, R4940, R4942 (1646–1648)**: R4937, R4940 and R4942 read with the Bethlen family key recovered ciphertext-only; R4939, in a different paired alphabet, broken ciphertext-only and read (23 Oct 2026). The entry is narrowed to R4936 (decoded, not read), R4938, R4941 and R4933–R4935. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/transylvania1645.html).
- **12 — Matignon in Mayenne–Forget's Cipher-2, BnF fr. 15572 ff. 218–219**: deciphered in part by Harumi-Matsuba with Tomokiyo's Cipher-2 table (issue #49, 19 Oct 2026); with two more Cipher-2 letters of fr. 15573 (ff. 120, 385; Harumi-Matsuba and Brett Burford) it is the entry mayenne1586c2, 86.1% in sense runs, measured here; removed 21 Oct 2026. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/mayenne1586c2.html).
- **310 (part) — Melchior de Sabran to Claude Bouthillier, Genoa 1633, BnF fr. 4134 ff. 46v–73v**: nine leaves deciphered in part with George Lasry's 1631 Sabran key by Harumi-Matsuba (issue #56, AI-only transcription, no human check), measured here 21 Oct 2026 at 879 of 891 symbols valued and about 74% of letters in words. Still open: most of f. 50v, stretches of ff. 54r and 57r, five name codes; fr. 4135 stays in the keys-in-hand list. 22 Oct 2026: George Lasry deciphered all imaged leaves of fr. 4134 and 27 of fr. 4135 with his key (PR #84); the entry now covers the whole Louis XIII–Sabran cipher. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sabran1633.html)
- **291 (part) — Jo. Antonio Bilia to Francesco II Sforza, Burgos, 22 Nov 1527, BnF fr. 3013 no. 43 ff. 102r–v**: f. 102r deciphered with Bilia's own Sforza key DECODE R5258 ("Cum equite Bilie") by Harumi-Matsuba (issue #55, AI-only transcription, no human check), checked here 21 Oct 2026 with the project's copy of the key (440 of 459 values agree); f. 102v (seven cipher lines) and the name code B.58 stay open. **No. 42** (Bilia to the ambassador Jo. Francesco Taverna, "Zaberna", same date, ff. 99r–v, Gallica views 159–162) is in a different cipher that neither R5258 nor R5237 fits: it stays open and needs cryptanalysis, not a key lookup. Removed from the keys-in-hand list 21 Oct 2026. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/bilia1527.html)
- **170 and 189 — Hieronimo Ranzo's letters, BnF fr. 2988 ff. 9–10 (DECODE R1894) and its copy fr. 20506 ff. 136–138 (R4450)**: key recovered from the ciphertext by George Lasry and, independently, Paolo Rosson (6 Oct 2026); measured here 18 Oct 2026, ff. 9–10 97.0%, f. 2 96.2%; removed 21 Oct 2026. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ranzo1528.html).
- **25 — Marie de Médicis to Savary de Brèves, 15 Sept and 10 Nov 1610 (BnF fr. 3789; R2075/R2076)**: deciphered by Brice Dudout with the Henri IV–Brèves key, BnF fr. 3462 view 112 (not 3642); checked here, 95 of 98 glyphs supported. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/breves1610.html).
- **190 — Two Sforza letters of 4 May 1446: the reply to Zorzo del Maino (BnF italien 1583 f. 68; DECODE R7898) and Amidani to Sforza (f. 70, on R7899)**: George Lasry recovered the key from the ciphertext with CTTS, refined by Astra and Fable 5.1 (PR #60), 20 October 2026; 721 of 827 cipher signs give sense, 14 sign classes and the f. 70 signature open. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/it1583.html).
- **298 — Guidi di Bagno to the Secretariat, 5 January 1652 (R5622)**: key recovered from ciphertext-only by George Lasry, 20 October 2026. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/bagno1652.html).
- **151 — Rodrigo Niño to Charles V, Naples, 19 March 1522 (BNE MSS/20213/27; DECODE R1182)**: George Lasry recovered the key with CTTS cryptanalysis; eight lines deciphered, one sign class ambiguous. Related prior published system acknowledged; exact-letter coverage unconfirmed. [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/nino1522.html)

- **36 — Frederick II's ministerial rescript to Louis Michell, Berlin 28 Dec 1751, KHA Prins Willem V inv. 198 (DECODE R1955)**: read in part by WAINUT (PR #29, 11 Oct 2026) in Michell's envoy code, with a table from the contemporary Dutch decipherments of R1050/R1051: 95 of 360 groups (26.4%), fragments only; the rest needs further decipherments of Michell's reports (not online) or NA 1.01.50 inv. 159 (not digitised). Split 14 Oct 2026 from michell1751 and removed. [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r1955.html)
- **296 — Walsingham's 1581 enclosure to Somers, TNA SP 53/11 no. 50 (DECODE R3839)**: found already solved, 23 Sept 2026. Norbert Biermann and George Lasry solved the cipher in 2023; Satoshi Tomokiyo published the solution on 16 Sept 2026 (cryptiana mary3.htm): "The beirar is domestik servand to the erle of shrevisbvry" and a glyph key with some signs unidentified. R3840 is not the ciphertext (extracts of Mary's letters, CSP Scotland vi 46-47), and the entry's "Portugal → France" / French came from the unrelated L'Abbadia worksheet (R3837). Checked and the opening verified on the image; nothing added. Notes in [`targets/walsingham1581/`](targets/walsingham1581/), removed 10 Oct 2026.
- **35 — Nicolas Desmarets (Marly) to an unnamed correspondent, 4 June 1710 (DECODE R10198)**: read with Tomokiyo's 1710 code table (Cryptiana 11B) applied to the 471 groups as one stream, 456 of 471 (96.8%); issue #28 (yuanyi-350) corrected the first attempt, which had aligned line by line. Removed 9 Oct 2026. [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/desmarets1710.html)
- **333 — Letters to Hetman Sieniawski and "Dresden" to Schenck (DECODE R7468–R7500)**: the fifteen Schenck letters are from an agent in Vienna, March–July 1706; their letter table (a = 7 8 9 … in alphabetical blocks of three) was recovered from the ciphertext alone, 93% of its numbers read, 63.6% of all cipher tokens with 65 of 77 three-digit codes open. The Sieniawski letters are mostly in print (Kaczka 2012); the six unprinted pieces carry contemporary decipherments, transcribed. Read in part, removed 9 Oct 2026. Split 14 Oct 2026, one key per entry: [Schenck letters](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/schenck1706.html), [Brâncoveanu and Cantacuzino, key A](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sieniawski1708.html), [Turculeț key B](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/turculet1709.html), [R7475](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r7475.html), [Chomentowski syllabary](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/chomentowski1713.html).

- **341 — Hamburg despatch to the Hessian chancellor, 4/14 May 1672, HStAM 4 f Dänemark Nr. 125 ff. 2–4 (HCPortal 494)**: read in part, 9 Oct 2026. The 26 code numbers carry a second hand's glosses; both letter-cipher passages deciphered with the letter table of HCPortal key 255 (HStAM 4 d Nr. 1234, Lincker 1666/1676); 59 of 64 cipher tokens (92.2%), five signs (e r t a z) make no word; blocked by the missing 1672 key. [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/hesse1672.html)
- **183 — Joachim de Dinteville (Langres) to the Duke of Nevers, BnF Français 3621 no. 116 f. 130 (DECODE R9451)**: key rebuilt from the interlinear decipherment of the sibling f. 128 by Alex Zarco (PR #24), completing NoAutopilot's partial key; 514 of 522 cipher tokens read (98.5%), verified and removed 7 Oct 2026. The letter is dated 4 July, not 3.

- **284 - Desiderio l’Abbé to Nevers, Prague and Breslau, 2 Mar and 2 May 1577** (BnF fr. 3198 nos. 30, 37, 38): key recovered from the ciphertext by AngusRobinson (PR #20), checked here; all 31 runs deciphered, 534 of 536 tokens. Removed 7 Oct 2026. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/labbe1582.html).
- **338 - Malsburg, 7/17 January 1637, alphabetic block** (HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411, image f. 12; HCPortal 497): period-five key recovered by Larry Beck with ChatGPT, with a partial critical reading. Prepared 2 Oct 2026. Alphabet `abcdefghiklmnopqrstuwxyz`, encryption shifts 4, 5, 3, 6, 2; 523 alphabetic positions in the unchanged normalized transcription and 510 in the selected image-based branch. Groups 43, 45 and the angular sign labelled LL remain unidentified. The tentative annotation `[E?]: Sixt: / cla:` has no established expansion or attribution. The earlier numerical-cipher work and its measure remain separately recorded. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/malsburg1637.html#letter-block), `targets/malsburg1637/alphabetic/`.

- **264 (R1873 part) — Alvise Mocenigo (Rome) to Doge Lorenzo Priuli, 25 April 1558, ASVe Busta 30 ff. 4–6 (DECODE R1873)**: found already deciphered, 22 Sept 2026; removed 2 Oct 2026. Paolo Bonavoglia (crittologia.eu, "Decifra dispacci veneziani") gives the transcription and full decipherment. The entry had merged it with R1871 (Foscarini, Paris, 11 September 1680) under DECODE metadata copied from a neighbour (not England, not 1555); on 14 Oct 2026 the two were split, and 264 is back in the open list narrowed to R1871 (attempted, open: Bonavoglia's decipherment offline, a ciphertext-only attempt here did not read it). [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r1873.html), [targets/r1873/](targets/r1873/NOTES.md).

- **259 — Philip II to the nuncio Ormanetto, copy sent to the Cardinal of Como (two cipher passages), ASV Spagna 7 ff. 303-304 (DECODE R116)**: read in part, 1 Oct 2026 (about 90%). Both passages are Italian, in the Spain nunciature's ordinary cipher, whose key was reconstructed by Ajaydas Devadas (email) and verified here on our own transcription (−3.0 per letter against −6.7 for 200 shuffled keys): the King on joining the Pope's league with the Emperor, and on Pitigliano. About 15 code groups open. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ormanetto1573.html), `targets/ormanetto1573/`, removed 1 Oct 2026.

- **239 — Nuncios Ormanetto and Antonio Clementino (Spain) to the Secretariat, ASV Spagna 10 (DECODE R117, R118)**: read 1 Oct 2026. R118 reads in Italian with the same key (the nuncio has told Secretary Pérez what Rome wrote in the cipher of 19 Sept; dateline 15 October, year 1576 or 1577 open); the clear note on the leaf is the contemporary decipherment, which the 21 Sept crib test had misread. R117 is a clear Spanish memorial, not a cipher. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ormanetto1576.html), `targets/ormanetto1576/`, removed 1 Oct 2026.

- **348 — Szembek papers: Latin cipher letter, Biblioteka Kórnicka BK 1560 (WBC edition 343124)**: read at the time, 28 Sept 2026. Not a Szembek letter: the postscript of a letter from the Wallachian court after the death of Prince Şerban Cantacuzino (Oct 1688), denouncing the imperial Jesuit envoy Antide Dunod. Every two-digit cipher run but one carries a contemporary interlinear decipherment; key rebuilt from the glosses (22 numbers) and every run checked; code group 202 open. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/szembek1689.html), `targets/szembek1689/`, removed 28 Sept 2026.

- **340 — Hessian polyalphabetic message of 1824, HStAM 9 a Nr. 259 f. 249 (HCPortal 513)**: read in full, 28 Sept 2026. An intercepted invisible-ink message headed "Snell an A?ud"; the decipherer's note on the leaf gives the key letters bcdefg, and the table (Vigenère without j, b..g = +2..+7, columns running on past z into the signs 1–4) was rebuilt from it. Questions on Paul Follen, America, Fries, Oken, de Wette and a plan found at Wetzlar. `targets/hesse1824/`.

- **198 — Andrea del Burgo (Ferrara) to Chancellor Gattinara, 26 Oct 1527, AGS Estado leg. 1563 fol. 572 (DECODE R9970)**: not a cipher, 28 Sept 2026. The four scans are two drafts of a clear Spanish minute of Charles V "al príncipe" (Prince Philip), 1543–1554, on an exchange of 110,770 ducats with Anton Fugger. The Burgo letter cited by Galende 1994 p. 163 is not on the record and was not located. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/burgo1527.html), removed 28 Sept 2026.

- **346 — Letters to Jobst Hermann von Ilten, 1697–1706, GWLB Hannover Ms XXIII 1245**: read at the time, 24 Sept 2026. Kalliope's note is "Dechiffrierter Brief" (deciphered), not "not deciphered"; the volumes are online in the GWLB digital library and every code run carries its interlinear decipherment. Transcribed and checked in `targets/ilten1697/`. The two 1743 letters to J. G. von Ilten in the scan's list (Ms XXIII 1234:29,2) were deciphered with the code table in Ms XXIII 1234:31,1 (`targets/ilten1743/`).

- **290 — Cardinal Schiner to Henry VIII, Trent, 14 June 1516, BL Cotton Vitellius B XIX f. 131**: found already read, 23 Sept 2026. Albert Büchi and Traugott Schiess deciphered it and printed the letter in full in *Korrespondenzen und Akten zur Geschichte des Kardinals Matth. Schiner* II (1925), no. 541, the cipher in italics (L&P II 2044 had called it "not deciphered"); keys never printed, leaf not digitised. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/schiner1516.html)

- **334 — Benedict XIII: autograph letter with cipher lines, and a three-page instruction largely in cipher, Arxiu Capitular de Barcelona**: found already read, 23 Sept 2026. Meister's second-hand report (1906) is Francesc Climent's Schism papers, which Canon Sebastián Puig y Puig deciphered and printed in *Pedro de Luna, último papa de Aviñón* (1920): 14 cipher letters of 1399–1416, the deciphered passages in italics, key rebuilt from Climent's own glosses on three of them and never printed; p. 475 is a facsimile of one cipher page. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/benedict1399.html).

Item **280** narrowed to Mniszech R7524; nine Potocka letters removed to [potocka1714](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/potocka1714.html), alphabet recovered, readings with explicit gaps.


- **194 — "Unknown sender (France?) to Duc de Guise?, 1 Jan 1556", BnF fr. 20974 pp. 1–3 (DECODE R2276)**: read in part, 23 Sept 2026, 91.8% of 3,920 signs as sense. Not 1556 and not to Guise: a letter from Reims, 19 June 1589, in the Nevers-Piles cipher (Biermann 2021, unpublished), on the Reims chapter's postulation of Brulart and Frison; the 1556 date came from the Albany key pasted on its verso. Torn edges, ~25 single signs and ~80 words open. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r2276.html)

- **321 — Matheo de Segura to the Constable of Castile, Madrid, 8 August 1596, BnF es. 336 no. 99**: both cipher passages read 23 Sept 2026; all 125 signs proposed, 119 excluding six tentative values. Last undeciphered item on Tomokiyo’s es. 336 list after Lasry 2023; no independent key found. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/segura1596.html).

- **281 — Passano, London to Francis I, Clairambault 331 f. 156**, March 1530: surviving account read 23 Sept 2026, 386/402 signs coherent with 13 emendations. Earlier negative superseded; three rare signs and the copyist's omitted continuation remain open. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/joachim1530.html).
- **276 — “Mémoire en chiffre du XIIe décembre 1560”, BnF fr. 3157 no. 67**: solved 22–23 Sept 2026; the cipher is on ff. 152r–156v (not f. 158). Homophonic cursive-sign substitution broken ciphertext-only and read at 98.9%: an unnamed Catholic gentleman of the Agenais to the connétable on the Protestant rising (Clairac, Caumont, Biron at Bergerac, Navarre at Nérac, the Agenais deputies to Orléans). [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/memoire1560.html).
- **271 — Speculum cipher flyleaf, SCH R 201 (DECODE R4817)**: solved 23 Sept 2026; an undated alchemical annotation in the 1618 volume, not an identifiable letter. Rous’s prior partial reading completed; 79 signs assigned, 78 coherent without correction; GEFAE retained and Gefäß marked conjectural. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r4817.html).

- **283 — Henriette de Clèves and Renato Birago to the duke of Nevers, Lyon, September 1574, BnF fr. 3315 ff. 15–16, 21**: read in part, 23 Sept 2026. Birago's 28 September letter is complete in a clear Italian fair copy beneath its cipher flap. The duchess's 16 September letter has an interlinear French decipherment and a clear ending on f. 16r, but its two dense cipher pages are not continuously transcribed. The 1584 Nevers no. 2 key is related but not identical. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/nevers1574.html).

- **178 — George de Selve, bishop of Lavaur (Venice) to Francis I, 2 ciphertexts (DECODE R3697, R4232)**: read in part, 22 Sept 2026. R3697 is BnF fr. 3091 f. 25 (14 Sept 1535), not fr. 3045; R4232 is fr. 3045 ff. 42–49 (5 July 1536). Read with Tomokiyo's key table extended on the letters: the 1536 letter 99.1%, the 1535 letter 79.1% (94.9% outside the decipherer's struck runs, which the film scan cannot separate). In `targets/selve1535/`.

- **121 — Unknown sender to unknown recipient, 2 ciphertexts, BL Harley MS 1582 (DECODE R8501, R8505)**: split and closed, 22 Sept 2026. R8501 (ff. 71–72) is a Stafford 1586 despatch whose contemporary decipherment is on its own f. 72r, already read in `targets/harley1582r8500/`. R8505 (ff. 263–264) is the French memoir "L'Estat du Roy de Navarre & de son party en France", after 1580, identified here and deciphered here from the ciphertext alone: a homophonic alphabet of ~50 graphic signs in which the letter m is written as a group of dots. Read (95.1%); ten nomenclator numbers open. `targets/harley1582r8505/`

- **275 — Intercepted letter, Banér to Stålhandske, 29 Dec 1640 (Cipherbrain 2019; APUG 568 f. 239)**: read in part, 22 Sept 2026. Johan Banér to Torsten Stålhandske, Hof; intercepted copy in Kircher's correspondence. Homophonic two-digit key (70 values) rebuilt here from context cribs; none of the Swedish keys on DECODE fits. 246 of 274 cipher tokens (89.8%) read: *nach der Oberpfaltz und gegen die [442]*, *die in Schlesien agirende [361]*, *mit guten Fürsichtigkeit nachvolgen*. Open: a courier's name, a stretch corrupt in the only copy, 14 code words. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/baner1640.html), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **273 — Hugo de Moncada, from Monaco, to Charles V, 6 Oct 1524, BNE MSS/20213/12 (BNE Digital "Carta 9"; DECODE R1191)**: found already solved, 22 Sept 2026. The pencil note on f. 1r points to *Colección de documentos inéditos* XXIV (1854) pp. 417–419, which prints the secretariat's decipherment with one gap; the key was rebuilt here against the print and the gap reads "que le lleuase a España, diziendome muchas razones para ello". Three unprinted code groups and a line-end *99* open (428/442 signs). Write-up `moncada1524`.
- **287 — Isabella of Castile to the Duke of Estrada, 20 Aug 1503, AGS Estado, Tratados con Inglaterra leg. 4 ff. 65–67 (now Patronato Real leg. 53 doc. 69)**: read in part, 22 Sept 2026. The one cipher Bergenroth could not read is in Galende's printed *Cifra general de los Reyes Católicos*, and every code group reads with it. Three copies survive (20 Aug, Sept, 3 Oct), two of them filed by Bergenroth under nos. 380–381. The letter covers the French war on Roussillon and Fuenterrabía and asks Henry VII for 2,000 English foot, not the marriage. About half the letter-sign runs are unread at PARES resolution (see [targets/estrada1503/](targets/estrada1503/)).
- **263 — Unknown sender (Roma) to unknown recipient, 29 Apr 1628, ASVe IT ASVe 0045 010 Busta 27 f. 354 (DECODE R1862)**: read in part, 22 Sept 2026. A Venetian ambassador's despatch to the Doge. The cipher is R1874's; its key was rebuilt from that record's contemporary decipherment by EM alignment and reads this letter unchanged (~84% of the groups). Cardinal Barberini asked whether Palmanova was fortified and the Pontebba pass secured; the ambassador urged a papal legation for Mantua and Savoy. Open: page 2 lines 18–28 and 20 word codes. Write-up docs/r1862.html
- **168 — Johann Arndt (1555-1621) (Glücksbrunn) to unknown recipient, 18 Sep 1798, Beinecke Mellon MS 136 (DECODE R2873)**: read, 22 Sept 2026. Not a letter: the alchemical manuscript book *Das zweyte Silentium Dei* (1798), pseudonymously ascribed to Arndt. Its 31 cipher passages (pp. 24–76) are in a Rosicrucian pigpen alphabet whose key is drawn on p. 83; all read with it, 1,545 signs, 2 unread. Page 24 had been read online by Lasry and Kopal (Schmeh, Cipherbrain, March 2022). See [targets/mellon136/](targets/mellon136/).
- **181 — Guido Ramgone (Venise) to Anne de Montmorency, 10 Jan 1530, BnF Français 3070 fol. 81 (DECODE R4249)**: read in part, 22 Sept 2026. Guido Rangone; homophonic cipher of about fifty signs broken ciphertext-only, with the sibling letter of 24 Dec 1529 (fr. 3082 f. 42, found through the Clairambault copies); 232 of 264 signs read (87.9%). Both passages concern Cesare Fregoso and Venice's offers to him. Four short runs of once-occurring signs open. `targets/rangone1530/`
- **163 — Cornelio Sperantio to ..., 2 ciphertexts, BayHStA Kurbayern Äußeres Archiv 4591 (DECODE R9411, R9412)**: read in part, 22 Sept 2026. Sperantio is the addressee of the cover, not the sender: R9411 is King John Zápolya to the dukes of Bavaria, Buda 6 Feb 1534 (mines, Nicolaus Freiberger), read 96% from its interlinear decipherment; R9412 a German newsletter from Buda, 8 Feb 1534 (Werbőczy's peace with the Turk), read 95%. Same key as R9415. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sperantio1534.html)
- **122 — Unknown sender to ..., Feb 1572, BL Harley MS 260 f 419 (DECODE R8364)**: read in part, 22 Sept 2026. Walsingham's letter-book copy of Burghley to Walsingham, early 1573 (Digges 1655 p. 327). The six-sign place name reads VALERY (St Valery), the same word as in four letters printed by Digges and in R8363; a nine-sign run and three person codes stay open, the 1571 key (SP 106/2 f. 141A) and the original (Cotton Vespasian F VI) not being online. See [targets/r8364/](targets/r8364/).
- **165 — … to unknown recipient, 1619, BayHStA Kurbayern Äußeres Archiv 4591 f 281 (DECODE R9426)**: read, 22 Sept 2026. Ferdinand of Bavaria, Elector-Archbishop of Cologne, to his brother Duke Maximilian I, Bonn, 22 December 1619 — the sender, the recipient, the place and the language were all recovered from the text. A homophonic cipher on the even figures 10–78 with a three-digit nomenclator, broken ciphertext-only; 95.8% of the cipher tokens read; the key also reads the sibling R9425 unchanged. Winter quarters and pay for the Catholic League's troops in Westphalia. Open: 31 of 32 nomenclator groups. Write-up docs/ferdinand1619.html
- **185 — Unknown sender (Rome) to Louis Gonzaga, duke of Nevers, 2 ciphertexts, 27 Mar – 4 Apr 1587, BnF fr. 3975 ff. 28, 30 (DECODE R4285, R4286)**: read, 22 Sept 2026. Two clear letters with seven cipher items, read with Nevers key no. 11 (fr. 3995 f. 23) once the writer's glyph forms were learnt from his glossed Clermont letter (fr. 3413 f. 102). Not Rome: the first is dated in cipher *De Randan* (Auvergne); Épernon's alarm passing *pres Lyon et ce pais*; codes 94 marquis, 6 Entragues, 23 queen of Navarre, 45 Épernon. 26 of 27 units accounted for (two struck out, one probable null). See [targets/nevers1587/](targets/nevers1587/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **173 — Unknown sender (Villeroy) to unknown recipient, 5 Feb 1587, BnF fr. 15564 fol. 30 (DECODE R4157)**: read, 22 Sept 2026. Catherine de Médicis to Villeroy, Chenonceau, 7 Feb 1581 (docket misread as 1587), printed in *Lettres de Catherine de Médicis* t. VII pp. 349–353. The eight cipher lines on f. 33r (deciphered at the time in the margin) read sign by sign with Tomokiyo's reconstructed key extended by twelve values (18 = dict, 4/b = nous, codes mais, la, Angleterre); his unread last line is *d'Angleterre* plus padding. Notes in targets/r4157/.
- **186 — Unknown sender to unknown recipient, 6 Jun 1588, BnF fr. 3976 fol. 133-134 (DECODE R3708)**: read 22 Sept 2026. The same Paris informant as ff. 62 and 131, to a member of Nevers's household; all 18 cipher runs read with the f. 62 key: the League municipality three weeks after the Barricades, La Chapelle reporting to Guise's council, Cotteblanche refusing the Arsenal's guns for Corbeil. See [targets/r3708/](targets/r3708/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **164 — Augurellio to …, BayHStA Kurbayern Äußeres Archiv 4591 ff. 232–235 (DECODE R9407)**: read, 22 Sept 2026 (96.7%; second pass read the overlooked image P6 = f. 235r). "Augurellio" is the cover address; the writer signs in cipher twice, *Iorg Weinmaister* — a Bavarian agent in Hungary, writing to Wilhelm IV and Ludwig X in spring 1533 (the siege of Palota; the docket reads "10 Decembris 33"). System A′ of the volume, key carried over from the sibling R9410's gloss and extended; f. 233 reads 95.7%, f. 235 96.1%, the slip and subscription 92%, the f. 234 column 65%. László Móré at Palota, the diet of Pressburg, Török Bálint, Simontornya, 50,000 Turks from Belgrade on Ofen. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/augurelio1535.html), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **182 — Unknown sender to unknown recipient, 3 ciphertexts, 11 Jan 1520, BnF fr. 3092 ff. 101–107 (DECODE R3699–R3701)**: resolved 22 Sept 2026; not published here at George Lasry's request (a reading with a historian is in preparation). See [targets/fr3092/](targets/fr3092/).
- **45 — Ferenc Wesselényi to unknown recipient, 1 Jan 1664, MNL OL E 199 8. pallium 1. 01 (DECODE R674)**: read, 22 Sept 2026. A Latin draft to the King (Leopold I), c. 1663–64, with sixteen number runs in a reversed alphabet (24 = a … 3 = x) plus vowel homophones 25–29, broken ciphertext-only; the alphabet is that of DECODE key R672. 241 of 244 tokens read; codes 60 and 64 open. DECODE's date is the start of a 1664–1670 span. See [targets/wesselenyi1664/](targets/wesselenyi1664/).
- **210 — Johann Valentin Andreae to unknown recipient, Embassy of the Free Mind (DECODE R4502)**: already read, 22 Sept 2026. Not a letter: a printed copy of the *Chymische Hochzeit* (Strassburg 1616); the four cipher pages are the novel's inscriptions, two simple substitutions glossed in the text itself (pp. 96, 98) and two symbolic dates (1378, 1459, per Foxcroft 1690). Sign table rebuilt. See [targets/andreae1616/](targets/andreae1616/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **235 — Unknown sender (Strängnäs) to unknown recipient, Carolina Rediviva (DECODE R4349)**: read in part, 22 Sept 2026. Uppsala UB C 513 holds two ciphers: the dated colophon on f. 19v (1517, each vowel written as the next letter), read and already printed in the Uppsala catalogue (1992); and the six signs DECODE means, closing an Old Swedish note to Anders Larsson pasted on f. 1r, too short to read. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r4349.html), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **115 — Edouard Lee Almosinar (Hispania) to unknown recipient, 1527, BL Cotton MS Vespasian C IV f. 156 (DECODE R8551)**: not a cipher, 22 Sept 2026. Both images viewed: Edward Lee's (the King's almoner) own clear English letter to Wolsey, Valladolid 5 June 1527, the closing leaf of *L&P* IV no. 3152 (Prince Philip's christening, the Pope reported dead, Pompeius Colonna against the Pope). A struck-through passage is ordinary script. See [targets/lee1526/](targets/lee1526/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **234 — Unknown sender (Please Select (only U.S. / Can / Aus)) to unknown recipient, Folkrörelsearkivet i Västerbotten (DECODE R1818)**: skipped as impossible, 22 Sept 2026. DECODE name 'Sami'; one small sheet, both sides filled (~7,500 signs) with a homemade cipher of Latin letter shapes, invented signs and numerals, comma word dividers, not plain Swedish. The only scan (~125 px/cm) is too coarse for a sign-accurate transcription; no key, sibling record or reference on DECODE, in print or online. Needs a 600 dpi rescan or physical access at Umeå. See [targets/r1818/](targets/r1818/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **120 — N. Wottoy ? to unknown recipient, Jun 1554, BL Harley MS 1582 ff. 8-10 (DECODE R8499)**: read in part, 22 Sept 2026. Dr Nicholas Wotton to Queen Mary I, Soissons, 13 June 1554; key DECODE R354 (SP 106/2 f. 162); 75.0% of 2,801 non-null signs firm, 89.4% incl. tentative; the contemporary deciphered copy ff. 5r-7v is not digitised. See [targets/harley1582r8499/](targets/harley1582r8499/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **267 — Unknown sender to Paulo Borne?, ÖStA HHStA Chiffrenschlüssel Kt. 13 Fasc. 20 ff. 38-39 (DECODE R1212)**: read at the time, 22 Sept 2026. Pál Bornemisza, Bishop of Transylvania, to Ferdinand I on the Kendi treason (c. 1556); interlinear decipherment, key R1211 on the next record, every run re-read; codes 24, 15, 10, 41, 70, 55, 78, 18, 52 open. See [targets/r1212/](targets/r1212/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **270 — Unknown sender to unknown recipient, 1 Jan 1664, ÖStA HHStA Verschwörerakten Fasc. 327 Konv. D ff. 30-31 (DECODE R688)**: not a cipher, 22 Sept 2026. The 'graphic signs' are Cyrillic: a clear Serbian letter of a prisoner, Stojan, asking to be got out of the German prison. See [targets/r688/](targets/r688/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **179 — Galeas Visconti (Alexandria) to Angelo Bolano / Francis Ist, 2 ciphertexts, BnF fr. 3045 f. 28 and fr. 3096 f. 91 (catalogued as "Français 3045 (2 items)"; DECODE R4230, R4256)**: read 22 Sept 2026 with Lasry's 2023 key, extended; 1408 of 1477 signs (95.3%). R4230 = fr. 3045 f. 28, to Bolano, Alessandria 15 Oct 1528 (95.2%); R4256 = fr. 3096 f. 91, to Francis I, pieve de Locate 15 June 1529 (95.4%); both closes read XV, not 12. Codes 48, rr, qq, 42, ep open. See [targets/visconti1529/](targets/visconti1529/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **187 — Unknown sender to Louis Gonzaga, duke of Nevers, 1 Jan 1588, BnF fr. 3976 fol. 62 (DECODE R3705)**: read 22 Sept 2026. Letter of a court informant in Paris dated 29 April 1588 (DECODE's date is a placeholder); 19 cipher runs read with a key rebuilt from the glossed sibling f. 131 of the same informant: Henri III tempted to have five or six Paris ligueurs drowned, Guise's threat of 40,000 men. See [targets/nevers1588/](targets/nevers1588/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **172 — Unknown sender [Guise?] to the duc de Mercœur, 27 May and 20 Jun 1587, BnF fr. 15564 ff. 119, 142 (DECODE R4162, R4165)**: read 22 Sept 2026 with Lasry's 2022 fr. 15564 key, extended; 95.1% of 2,477 letter signs: League levies (Pfyffer's Swiss, Italian lances, reiters), the Protestant army of 1587, a meeting at Meaux. See [targets/guise1587/](targets/guise1587/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **124 — W. Baurleighs? to unknown recipient, 27 Jan 1572, BL Harley MS 260 f. 395 (DECODE R8362)**: resolved 22 Sept 2026. Not a cipher: Walsingham's letter-book copy of Burghley to Walsingham, Westminster 24 Jan 1572/3, printed in Digges 1655; the two boxed 9s mark the cover-names Lyons and Roome. See [targets/r8362/](targets/r8362/).
- **177 — Unknown sender (Campo a Landriano) to Anne de Montmorency, 30 Aug 1528, BnF fr. 3034 fol. 154 (DECODE R4223)**: read in part 22 Sept 2026. The sender is Galeazzo Visconti (his cipher; Lasry's 2023 key table "3045f28 and 3034f154"); 1247 of 1337 signs read with Lasry's key extended: the League's council of war at Landriano, Milan or Pavia. Numbered codes 48, 4θ, 4r, rr, qq, peu open. See [targets/visconti1528/](targets/visconti1528/), removed 22 Sept 2026.
- **171 — Unknown sender to Philippe-Emmanuel, duke of Mercoeur, 3 ciphertexts, BnF fr. 15564 (DECODE R4155, R4158, R4167)**: f. 78 (R4158, 16 Apr 1587, probably Guise) read 22 Sept 2026 with Lasry's 2022 key, extended; f. 27 (R4155) already read by Lasry; f. 151 (R4167) is catalogue 13, closed in [targets/mercoeur1586/](targets/mercoeur1586/). See [targets/mercoeur1587/](targets/mercoeur1587/).
- **175 — Unknown sender to Francis I (King of France), 11 Jan 1530, BnF fr. 3029 ff. 134–135 (DECODE R3670)**: resolved 22 Sept 2026; not published here at George Lasry's request (a reading with a historian is in preparation). See [targets/fr3029f134/](targets/fr3029f134/).
- **136 — ... (Paris) to unknown recipient, 19 Apr 1641, BL Harley MS 7001 ff. 148–149 (DECODE R7766)**: read in part 22 Sept 2026. Robert Reade, Windebank's nephew, to his cousin; read with the Windebank key reconstruction DECODE R9115 (Add MS 32256 f. 4). See [targets/harley7001/](targets/harley7001/).
- **145 — Jerónimo Adorno? to unknown recipient, undated, RAH 9/26 ff. 371–376 (DECODE R9658)**: deciphered at the time and in print, 22 Sept 2026. It is Lope Hurtado de Mendoza to Charles V, Rome, 23 December 1522, in Lope Hurtado's 1522 cipher: Bergenroth, *CSP Spain* ii no. 510 (Salazar A. 26, ff. 371–377, "Autograph in cipher. Contemporary deciphering."), on the Pope's refusal of a league while a general peace is hoped for, the Swiss envoys' demand for 36,000 ducats and England's threat of war in Picardy. All six images viewed; the decipherment is not among them. See [targets/r9658/](targets/r9658/).
- **39 — Gottfried van Swieten (Bonn) to Count Cobenzl, 3 ciphertexts, 1757–59, ARA Brussels SEG 1236 (DECODE R955–R957)**: read 22 Sept 2026. R955 deciphered on the page; the 1759 alphabetical syllabary rebuilt ciphertext-only, 96% read. See [targets/swieten1757/](targets/swieten1757/).
- **117 — Sr. Ed. Stafford to unknown recipient, 9 Nov 1586, BL Harley MS 1582 ff. 76–77 (DECODE R8504)**: read 22 Sept 2026. Clear holograph letter to [Walsingham]; one cipher word, *princes*, read with the R8500 sibling key.
- **203 — Antonio Muxetula, ambassador in Rome, to Charles V, 24 Oct 1531, AGS Estado leg. 853 doc. 91 (DECODE R9962; whole letter R9961)**: read at the time, 22 Sept 2026. The decipherment is bound with the letter as doc. 91 bis (AGS scans 021-023, paragraphs A, b, C, d keyed to the letter's margins; two more blocks glossed in the margin). Key Luo Cifrario 22. Content: the French *vistas* dropped after the Regent's death, England urging France to war, the Pope's advice on the Diet of Speyer and the Emperor's route home. See [targets/muxetula1531/](targets/muxetula1531/).
- **204 — Unknown sender (Rome) to High Commander of León, 12 Sep 1531, AGS Estado leg. 853 fol. 85 (DECODE R9960)**: not a cipher, 22 Sept 2026.
